                             A   !ilL!.:.
          FOR AN ORDINANCE RELATING TO ZONING, DIVIDING
          THE CITY AND COUNTY OF DENVER INTO DISTRICTS
          AND SPECIFYING FOR SUCH DISTRICTS THE PERMIT-
          TED USES FOR STRUCTURES AND TRACTS OF LAND,
          FIXING SIZE, BULK AND LOCATION LIMITATIONS ON
          STRUCTURES CONTAINING SUCH USES, ESTABLISHING
          REQUIREMENTS FOR OFF-STREET PARKING AND LOAD-
          ING FACILITIES AND ENUMERATING RESTRICTIONS
          ON THE LOCATION, SIZE AND CHARACTER OF SIGNS;
          PROVIDING FOR ADMINISTRATION, ENFORCEMENT AND
          AMENDMENT AND FOR JUDICIAL REVIEW; AMENDING
          AND RE-ENACTING CHAPTER I OF MAIN DIVISION 6,
          610. ZONING, AND ALL THE VARIOUS SECTIONS AND
          SUB-SECTIONS THEREOF, OF THE REVISED MUNICIPAL
          CODE, AS AMENDED.
DE IT ENACTED BY THE OOUNCIL OF THE CITY AND COUNTY OF DENVER:
   SECTION 1.    Chapter I of Main Division 6, 610. Zoning, and the
various sections and sub-sections thereof, of the Revised Munici-
pal Code, as amended, is hereby amended and re-enacted as amend-
ed to read as follows:

 . 1-l. PURPOSE OF ORDINM-.JCE .
 • l-2 •SEVERABILITY OF SECTIONS OF ORDINANCE .
 • l-3. STATUTE OF LIMITATIONS.
 . l-4. DISTRICT REGULATIONS TO BE UNIFORM. EA~EPTION .
 . l-5. EFFECT OF OTHER ORDINANCES AND REGULATIONS •
 • l-6. EFFECT OF PRIVATE COVENANTS .
 • l-7. LEGISLATIVE CONDITIONS PRESERVED .
 • l-8. OFFENSES AND LIABILITIES PRESERVED .
 . l-9. PENDING ACTIONS AND PROCEEDINGS .
 .l-10. TERMINATION OF AUTHORIZATIONS TO ERECT, ALTER, OCCUPY
        OR USE STRUCTURES. EXCEPTION .

         . 1-1.   £URPOSE OF ORDINANCE.     This ordinance is enacted to
 preserve and promote the public health, safety and welfare of
 the inhabitants of the City and County of Denver, and of the pub-
 lic generally, and to encourage and facilitate the orderly growth
 and expansion of the municipality .
        • 1-2.    SEVERABILITY OF SECTIONS OF ORDINANCE.     If for any rea-
 son any one or more sections, sentences, clauses or parts of this
 ordinance shall be held invalid, such judgment shall not affect,
 impair or invalidate the remaining provisions of this ordinance
 but shall be confined in its operation to the specific sections,
 sentences, clauses or parts of this ordinance so held invalid and
 the invalidity of any section, sentence, clause or part of this

=== page 11 ===
                                                          48   155




ordinance in any one or more instances shall not affect or pre-

judice in any way the validity of this ordinance in any other
instance •
    . 1-3.     §18Iurg OF LIMITATlONS.     All actions by the municipality
based upon the unlawful location, bulk or maximum gross floor area
in a structure shall be brought within three years after the issu-
ance by the Department of Zoning Administration of a Certificate of
Use and Occupancy for such structure and not after that period •
    • 1-4.     DISTRICT REGULATIONS TO BE UNIFORM. EXCEPTION.        The
regulations herein established shall apply uniformly to all geo-
graphical areas having the same district classification and bear-
ing the same symbol or designation on the map or maps incorpora-
ted in and made a part of this ordinance; provided, however, that
where a district boundary line divides an area of land designated
as a single Zone Lot pursuant to the provisions of this ordinance
and such entire area of land was held in one ownership of record
on the date the district boundary line was established, either
by this ordinance or by an amendment hereto, then and in that
event:
   .1-4(1).       Each parcel of land of the area so divided may be
used in conformity with and subject to the regulations herein
established for the district in which each such parcel of land
is located; or
   .1-4(2).       If the area is divided into two or more parcels of
land, the entire area may be used in conformity with and subject
to the regulations herein established for the district in which
is located the parcel of land, if any such parcel there be,
which contains more than one-half of the area of the entire Zone
Lot; or
   .l-4(3).       If the area is divided into two equal parcels of
land,    th~   entire area may he 11~ed in conformity with and subject




                                     -2-

=== page 12 ===
48   156


to the regulations herein established for the distr.Ct in which
either of such parcels of land is located; provided, however,
     .l-4(4).              That no parcel of land containing more than 6,250
square feet shall be used in any manner except in conformity
with and subject to the regulations herein established for the
district in which such parcel of land is located.
     .1-5.                                      -
              EFFECT OF OTHER ORDINANCES AND REGULATIONS.
              _.....,...                                       --    Wherever
the provisions of any other applicable statute, local ordinance
or regulation establish higher or more restrictive standards than
are established by the provisions of this ordinance, the provi-
sions of such other statute, local ordinance or regulation shall
govern •
     • l-6.   EFFEa_QF PRIVATE COVENANTS.             Nothing herein contained
shall be construed to render inoperative any restrictions estab-
lished by covenants running with the land and which restrictions
are not prohibited by or are not contrary to the regulations
herein established •
     • 1-7.   LEGISLATIVE CONDITIONS PRESERVED.           All conditions and
requirements established by Council incidental to the zoning or
rezoning of any area are hereby preserved and shall continue in
full force and effect .
     . 1-8.   OFFENSES AND LIABILITIES PRESERVED.           All offenses com-
mitted and all liabilities incurred prior to the effective date
of this ordinance shall be treated as though all prior zoning
ordinances and amendments thereto were in full force and effect
for the purpose of sustaining any proper suit, action or prose-
cution with respect to such offenses and liabilities .
     • 1-9.   PENDING ACTIONS AND PROCEEDINGS.           All pending judicial
actions and all proceedings pending before the Board of Adjustment
on the effective date of this ordinance shall be treated until
the conclusion of such actions and proceedings as though all prior
zoning ordinances and amendments thereto were in full force and
effect.
                                          -3-

=== page 13 ===
                                                     48   157



   .1-10.   l§MHJATION OF .AUTHORIZATIONS TO ERECT, ALTER, OC-

CUPY OR usg STRUCTURES, EXCEPTION.   Every authorization and
permission, of whatever kind and nature and however obtained,
heretofore granted under any prior zoning ordinance by the
municipality or any department, agency, officer or employee
thereof, to erect, alter, occupy or use any structure other
than in full compliance with all provisions of this ordinance
shall terminate and expire on the date this ordinance becomes
effective; provided, however, that nothing herein contained
shall require any change in the plans, construction or desig-
nated .use of a building for which a building permit has been
heretofore legally issued and the construction of which build-
ing shall have been actually begun within ninety days of the
date of such permit and the ground story framework of which
building, inclw ing the second tier of beams, shall have been
completed within one year of the date of such permit and which
entire building shall be completed, according to such plans
as are filed, within two years from the date this ordinance be-
comes effective.

           • 2-1.     CREATION.   There shall be and hereby is created a De-
     partment of Zoning Administration (hereinafter called the Depart-
     ment) •
           . 2-2.     MAN~ENT     AND STAFF.       The Zoning Administrator shall
     be the officer in full charge of the Department and shall devote
     his entire time to the duties of his office.               He shall be appoint-
     ed by the Mayor and shall hold his office at the pleasure of the
     Mayor.         The salary of the Zoning Administrator, his staff of
     assistants and the salaries of such assistants shall be fixed by
     Council.        No member of the staff of the Department, including
     the Zoning Administrator, shall be employed by or be a member of
     the Board of Adjustment •
           • 2-3.    DUTI£S~D     POWERS .
           • 2-3(1).    bdministr~tiQU.       There is hereby vested in the De-
     partment the duty of administering this ordinance and the power
     necessary for such administration, incidental to which duty and
     power the Department shall:
           .2-3(l)(a).      Intervene in all proceedings before the Board of
     Adjustment, present facts and information to assist the Board
     in reaching a decision, resist and oppose any deviation from the
     standard provisions of this ordinance and have decisions of the
     Board reviewed in a court of proper jurisdiction when, in the
     judgment of the Zoning Administrator, such review is desirable;
           .2-3(l)(b).     Propose to Council and recommend the enactment
     of amendments to this ordinance for the purpose of improving ad-
     ministration and enforcement;

        .2-3(2)(d).      Institute in courts of proper jurisdiction pro-

     and administrative determinations hereunder .
        . 2-4.    APPEALS FROM THE DEPARTMENT .
        . 2-4(1).    Procedure.   Any person aggr~ed or any officer or
     department of the municipality may appeal to the Board of Adjust-
     ment from any order or decision of the Department.     Such appeal
     shall be taken by filing with the Department and with the Board

                                                       48   161




of Adjustment, within the time provided by the rules of the Board,
a notice of appeal specifying the particular grounds upon which
the appeal is taken.     Upon recei~ of a notice of appeal, the De-
partment shall transmit to the Board of Adjustment all of the
original documents and material, or true copies thereof, consti-
tuting the record upon which the order or decision appealed from
was based •
   • 2-4(2).    £ffeci_of Appeal.    An appeal to the Board of Adjust-
ment shall stay all proceedings unless the Zoning Administrator
certifies that, by reason of the facts stated in the certificate,
a stay in his opinion would cause imminent peril to life or
property.      When such a certificate is filed, proceemngs shall
not be stayed except by a restraining order granted, after due
notice to the Department, by the Board of Adjustment or a court
or proper jurisdiction.

     .3.     BOARD OF ADJUSTMENT .
     . 3-1. CREATION .
     • 3-2. STAFF .
     • 3-3. RULES FOR PROCEEDINGS BEFORE BOARD .
     • 3-4. POWERS OF THE BOARD.
     ,3-4(1). Administrative Review.
     ,3-4(2). Variances •
     . 3-4(3). Oaths and Attendance of Witnesses .
     . 3-5. LIMITATIONS ON THE PmvERS OF TEE BOARD •

           • 3-1.   CREATlQtl. There shall be and hereby is created a Board
     of Adjustment (hereinafter called the Board) consisting of five
     members.       The members of the Board shall be appointed by Council
     for a term of five years and may be removed only for cause upon
     written charges and after public hearing.        The members of the
     Board serving on theafective date of this ordinnce, under a
     zoning ordinance effective prior hereto, shall be and constitute
     the first Board hereunder and ech member thereof shall serve the
     balance of the term to which he was appointed.        Any vacancy
     which occurs in the Board shall be filled by Council for the un-
     expired term of any member whose term became vacant.        The com-
     pensation of the members of the Board shall be fixed by Council.
     No member of the Board shall be on the staff of the Board or
     be employed in or by the Department of Zoning Administration •
           . 3-2.   STAFF.   The staff of the Board shall consist of a
     Technical-Secretary and such other assistants as may be autho-
     rized by Council.       The Technical-Secretary shall be the technical
     advisor to the Board and custodian of its records, shall conduct
     official correspondence and generally supervise the clerical and
     technical work of the Board.        He shall be appointed by the Board
     and shall devote his entire time to the duties of his office.
     The salary of the Technical-Secretary, the number of additional
     assistants and the salaries of such additional assistants shall

                                       -9-

=== page 19 ===
                                                      48   163




be fixed by Council.      No member of the staff shall be a member
of the Board or be employed in or by the Department of Zoning
Administration .
    • 3-3.   B!JLES FOR PROCEEDINGS BEEQRE BOARD.   The Board shall
adopt reasonable rules governing all proceedings before it.            Such
rules shall provide and require that:
    .3-3(1).     Public notice shall be given of all hearings and
all hearings shall be open to the public;
    .3-3(2).     Due notice of all hearings shall be given to parties
in interest and to the Department of Zoning Administration which
Department shall be permitted to intervene in all proceedings;
    .3-3(3).    At any hearing a representative of the Department
of Zoning Administration and any other interested party may
appear in person or by agent or by attorney, offer evidence and
testimony and cross-examine witnesses;
    .3-3(4).    All witnesses shall be sworn or shall affirm their
testimony in the manner required in courts of record;
    .3-3(5).    All evidence and testimony shall be presented
publicly.      The Board may take judicial notice of facts to the
same extent and in the same manner as courts of record and may
consider any relevant facts within the personal knowledge of
any member of the Board which are stated into the record by
such member;
    .3-3(6).    A stenographic record shall be made and trans-
scribed of all public proceedings.      The transcribed record
shall include, but shall not be limited to, the verbatim
testimony offered by all witnesses and all personal knowledge
of members of the Board considered by the Board in reaching
its decision.      Additionally, the record of proceedings shall
include all documents and physical evidence considered.          The
record of proceedings shall not include the deliberations of
the 2oard at executive sessions but shall show the vote of
each member upon each question, or, if absent or failing to

                                -10-

=== page 20 ===
48   164

 vote, shall indicate such fact.      The foregoing record of pro-
 ceedings shall be filed immediately in the office of the Board
 and shall be a public record .
     • 3-4.   ~ERS   OF TtlE BOABD.   Subject to all limitations enume-
 rated herein, the Board shall have and exercise the following

     .3-4(1).    Administrative Review.    Appellate jurisdiction of
 any decision or order made hereunder by the Department of Zoning
 Administration.     The Board may reverse or affirm, wholly or part-
 ly, or may modify the decision or order appealed from and may
 make such decision or order as ought to be made, and to that
 end shall have all the powers of the Department from which the
 appeal is taken •
     . 3-4(2}.   Vari~~~~.   Original jurisdiction to authorize, in
 a specific case, a variance from the terms of this ordinance,
 subject to terms and conditions fixed by the Board, as will not
 be contrary to the p~blic     inte~2st,   where, owing to exceptional
 or extraordinary circumstances, literal       enforc~ment   of the pro-
 visions of this ordinance would result in unnecessary hardship;
 provided, however, that no variance shall be granted unless the
 Board shall find th3t all of the following conditions exist:
     .3-4(2)(a).     That the variance will not authorize a use other
 than those uses which are specifically enumerated as Uses by
 Right for the district in which is located the property for which
 the variance is sought;
     .3-4(2)(b).     That the plight of the owner of the property for
 which the variance is sought is due to unique circumstances ex-
 isting on the property and that the unique circumstances were
 neither created by the owner of the property nor were due to or
 the result of general conditions in the district in which the
 property is located;
     .3-4(2)(c).     That the development or use of the property .

 f~r    which the variance is sought, if limited by a literal enforce-

 conditions required to exist for the authorization of a variance
 shall be construed as limitations on the power of the Board to

     . 3-6.   APPEAL.§ FROM THE_J2Q8RD .
     • 3-6(1).   Procedure.    Any person aggrieved, any taxpayer or
 any officer or department of the municipality may have a decision
of the Board reviewed in the manner provided by statute or by
rules relating to civil proceedings.            No such review shall be
granted unless a petition therefor, duly verified, setting forth
that such decision is illegal, in whole or in part, and specify-
ing the grounds of the illegality, is presented to a court of
proper jurisdiction within thirty days after the filing of the
decision in the office of the Board.           The Board shall not be re-
quired to return the original papers acted upon by it, but it
shall be sufficient to return certified or sworn copies thereof
or such portions thereof as may be called for.           The return shall
concisely set forth such other facts as may be pertinent and
material to the decision appealed from and shall be verified •
     • 3-6(2).   Effect of Appeal.    Granting a petition or the issu-
ance of a writ shall not stay proceedings upon the decision appeal-
ed from, but the court, on application after notice to the Board
and on due cause shown, may grant a restraining order .
     • 3-6(3).   Hearing on Appeal.       If upon the hearing it shall
appear to the court that testimony is necessary for the proper

disposition of the matter, it may take evidence or appoint a
referee to take such evidence as it may direct, and report the
same to the court with his findings of fact and conclusions of
law, which shall constitute a part of the proceedings upon which
the determination of the court shall be made.   The court may
reverse or affirm, wholly or partly, or may modify the decision
brought up for review.

 map delineating the various districts, together with all matters

       • 4-2.   LOCAIING QISTRICT BOllNQARY LINES NOT READILY DETERMIN-
 ABLE FROM THE ATTACHED MAP.        If for any reason the location of
 any district boundary line is not readily determinable from the
 attached map, the location of the district boundary line shall be
 fixed by the Department of Zoning Administration in accordance
 with the following provisions.        Where more than one of the follow-
 ing provisions are applicable in any given situation, the pro-
 vision with the lowest number shall prevail over allother pro-
 visions:
       .4-2(1).   Where a district boundary line is located with re-
 ference to a fixture or monument, the location of such fixture
 or monument shall control;
       .4-2(2).   VJhere a district boundary line is given a position
 within a street or alley right-of-way or channelized waterway,
 the district boundary line shall be deemed to be in the center of
 such street or alley right-of-way or channelized waterway;
       .4-2(3).   Where a district boundary line is shown as approxi-
 mately following platted lot lines, the district boundary line
 shall be deemed to coincide with such platted lot lines;
       .4-2(4).   Where a district boundary line is shown by a specific
 dimension, the specific dimension shall control;
       .4-2(5).   Where a district bou~ary line extends in the direc-
 tion of the length of a block, the district boundary line shall
be deemed to coincide with the center line of such block;
       .4-2(6).   Where a district boundary line divides a platted lot

       • 5-l.    PROHIBITIONS •
       • 5-1(1).      Limitations on Land and Structures.    No land or
structure shall be used and no structure shall be occupied, de-
signed, erected or altered except in conformity with all regula-
tions herein established and upon performance of all conditions
herein set forth •
       • 5-1(2).     Zoning ~rmit t£_grect or Alter Str~cty~        No
structure shall be erected or altered until a Zoning Permit for
such erection or alteration shall have been issued by the Depart-
ment of Zoning Administration .
       . 5-1(3).     Zoning Permit for Change of Use.   Neither the use
of or the uses upon any land nor the use of or the uses within
any structure shall be changed until a Zoning Permit for such
change shall have been issued by the Department of Zoning Admin-
istration •
       • 5-1(4).     Limitations on Administrative Agencies.    No permit,
certificate, license or other document or oral approval, the use
of which may be subject to the provisions of this ordinance,
shall be issued by any department, agency or board of the muni-
cipality until the Department of Zoning Administration shall have
certified that the use to be made of the permit, certificate,
license, other document or oral approval is in full compliance
with the provisions of this ordinance .
       . 5-2.    PENALTIES AND REMEDIES .
       • 5-2( 1) .   Violations.   vvherever by the provisions of this
ordinance the performance of any ac.;t is required or the perfor-

      mance of any act is prohibited or wherever any regulation, dimen-

       erection, construction, reconstruction, alteration, repair, con-
       version, maintenance or use, to restrain, correct or abate such
       violation, to prevent the occupancy of said building, structure
       or land or to prevent any illegal act, conduct, business or use
       in or about iuch premises.       The imposition of any penalty here-
       under shall not preclude the municipality or any proper person
       from instituting an appropriate action or proceeding in a court
       of proper jurisdiction to require compliance with the provisions
       of this ordinance and administrative orders or determinations
       made hereunder.

        • 1-1(1),   Enclosure of Us~.      Every use, unless expressly exem-
pted by this ordinance, shall be operated in its entirety within
a completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use from the
requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not

be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted,
        .1-1(2).    Volume of Sound Generated. Every use, unless express-
ly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated that the volillE
of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not exceed fifty
five decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on

or gases shall be stored in bulk above ground; provided, however,

   .1-2(3),             Accessory Uses.      Incidental only to a Use by Right,

   .l-2(3)(e).              If operated partially or entirely in detached struc-

     • 1-3(2).   Location of Structures.      Except as otherwise herein-

     • l-3(2)(a).    Front Setback.    All structures shall be set in a
distance of not less than twenty feet from each front line of the
Zone Lot; provided, however, that on the two shorter dimensions
 of any block oblong in shape, the front setback may be reduced
 to five feet for electric substations, gas regulator stations and
utility pumping stations and to ten feet for all other structures;
and provided, further, that detached atcessory~structures and

                                                         48   179




accessory uses, except garages, shall be set in a sufficient dis-
tance from each front line of the Zone Lot so that such structures
and uses are located only on the rear one-fourth of the Zone Lot.
On a corner Zone Lot, detached accessory structures and accessory
uses, except garages, shall be set in a sufficient distance from
each front line of the Zone Lot so that such structures and uses
are located only on the rear part of the Zone Lot which is adjacent
to and corresponding with the rear one-fourth of abutting Zone Lots
and are located no closer to the side street right-of-way than one-
half the dimension of the corner Zone Lot, measured perpendicularly
from such side street right-of-way •
   • l-3(2)(b).   Rear Setback.   If no alley abuts the rear line of
the Zone Lot, all detached accessory structures and fixtures shall
be set in a distance of not less than five feet and all other struc-

tures shall be set in a distance of not less than twenty feet from
the rear line of the Zone Lot.     If an alley abuts the rear line of
the Zone Lot, detached garages and carports opening directly on the
alley shall be set in a distance of not less than fivefuet from
the alley line; detached accessory structures (including garages
and carports which do not open directly on the alley) and fixtures
for the disposal of trash and garbage may be located on the alley
line and all other structures shall be set in a distance of not less
than twenty feet from the center line of the abutting alley.        Whether
or not an alley abuts the rear line of the Zone Lot, electric sub-
stations, gas regulator stations and utility pumping stations which
do not have a rear entrance may be located on the rear line of the
Zone Lot; if such structures do have a rear entrance, they shall be
set in a distance of not ~ss than five feet from the rear line of
the Zone Lot •

      .2-1.   LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES .
      • 2-1(1).   Enclosure of Uses.     Every use, unless expressly exempt-
ed by this ordinance, shall be operated in its entirety within
a completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use from the
requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not
be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted •
      • 2-1(2).   Volume of Sound Generated.        Every use, unless express-
ly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated that the volume
of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not exceed fifty
five decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on
which the use is located •
      • 2-1(3).   Vibration Generated.        Every use shall be so operated
that the ground vibration inherently and recurrently generated is
not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of any boundary
line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located •
      • 2-1(4).   Emission of Heat~are. Radiation and Fumes.           Every
~se    shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or dan-

   • 3-3(1).    Zone Lot for Structures •
   . 3-3(l)(a).    Designation and Maintenance of Zone Lot. A separate
ground area, herein called the Zone Lot,    shal~   be designated, pro-
vided and continuously maintained for each structure containing a
Use by Right.     Every application to erect or alter such a structure
shall contain an accurate description of the Zone Lot on which the
structure is to be located.     Thereafter, the designated Zone Lot
shall be continuously provided and maintained for the purpose and
for the structure designated and shall be used for no other purpose,
nor shall the boundaries or the area of the Zone Lot be changed;
provided, however, that upon application to and approval by the

Department of Zoning Administration, the boundaries and area of a
designated Zone Lot may be changed if, in lieu thereof, another Zone
Lot complying with all requirements herein can be designated, pro-

   • 3-3(1) (b).      IV1inimum Width and Size of Zone Lot. The Zone Lot
for each structure designed or used, either wholly or partly, for
residential occupancy shall be not less than fifty feet wide at
the front setback line for structur~and shall contain not less
than h,OOO square feet plus 3,000 square feet for each dwelling unit
in excess of two dwelling units located on the same Zone Lot •
   • 3-3(l)(c).       Unobstructed Open Space on Zone Lot.    On the same
Zone Lot for each of the following structure, there shall be pro-
vided at least the following amount of Unobstructed Open Space which
shall be unobstructed except by landscaping fixtures or permitted
fences:      (1)    Single unit dwelling - 4,000 square feet; (2)    Pre-
school - 4,000 square feet; (3)          Each dwelling unit in a multiple
unit dwelling - 1,500 square feet •
   • 3-3(2).       Location of Structures. Except as otherwise herein-
after provided, the space resulting from the following setbacks
shall be open and unobstructed •
   • 3-3(2)(a).       Front Setback.    All structures shall be set in a
distance of not less than twenty feet from each front line of the
Zone Lot; provided, however, that on the two shorter dimensions
of any block oblong in shape, the front setback may be reduced
to five feet for electric substations, gas regulator stations and
utility pumping stations and to ten feet for all other structures;
and provided, further, that detached accessory structures and
accessory uses, except garages, shall be set in a sufficient dis-
tance from each front line of the Zone Lot so that s~h structures
and uses are located only on the rear one-fourth of the Zone Lot.
On a corner Zone Lot, detadrerl acce~~o~y structures and accessory
uses, except gar·agec;, shall be set in a suffi cie11t distance from

       • 3-3(2)(b).   Rear Setback. If no alley abuts the rear line of
 the Zone Lot, all detached accessory structures and fixtures shall
 be set in a distance of not less than five feet and all other struc-
 tures shall be set in a distance of not less than twenty feet from
 the rear line of the Zone Lot.        If an alley a buts the rear line of
the Zone Lot, detached garages and carports opening directly on the
alley shall be set in a distance of not less than five feet from
the alley line; detached accessory structures (including garages
and carports which do not open directly on the alley) and fixtures
for the disposal of trash and garbage may be located on the alley
line and all other structures.shall be set in a distance of not less
than twenty feet from the center line of the abutting alley.             Whether
or not an alleyrouts the rear line of the Zone Lot, electric sub-
stations, gas regulator stations and utility pumping stations which
do not have a rear entrance may be located on the rear line of the
Zone Lot; if such structures do have a rear entrance, they shall be
set in a distance of not less than five feet from the rear line of
the Zone Lot .

     . 3-3(3).   Bulk of Structures.       No part of any structure (except
church spires, church towers, flagpoles, antennas, chimneys, flues,
vents or accessory water tanks) shall project up through bulk limits
which are defined by planes starting at horizontal lines which are
co-directional to the front, rear and side lines of the Zone Lot
and pass through points ten feet above the midpoint of each such
front, rear and side line and which planes extend up over the Zone
Lot at an angle of forty-five degrees with respect to the horizontal
(a pitch of one foot additional rise for each foot additional set-
back).      (See Graphic Fig. No. 1)

which the use is located •

     • 4-3.   PERMITTED STRUCTURES .
     •4-3(1).    Zone Lot for Structures •
     • 4-3(l)(a).   Designation and Maintenance of Zone Lot.     A   separate
ground area, herein called the Zone Lot, shall be designated, pro-
vided and continuously maintained for each structure containing a
Use by Right.       Every application to erect or alter such a structure
shall contain an accurate description of the Zone Lot on which the
structure is to be located.       Thereafter, the designated Zone Lot
shall be continuously provided and maintained for the purpose and
for the structure designated and shall be used for no other purpose,
nor shall the boundaries or the area of the Zone Lot be changed;
provided, however, that upon application to and approval by the
Department of Zoning Administration, the boundaries and area of a
designated Zone Lot may be changed if, in lieu thereof, another Zone
Lot complying with all requirements herein can be designated, pro-
vided and continuously maintained •
     • 4-3(l)(b).   Minimum Width and Size of Zone Lot.     The Zone Lot
for each structure designed or used, either wholly or partly, for
reskEntial occupancy shall be not less than fifty feet wide at
the front setback line for structur~and shall contain not less
than 6,000 square feet •

 after provided, the space resulting from the following setbacks
 shall be open and unobstructed .
    • 4-3(2)(a).    Front Setback.    All structures shall be set in a
 distance of not less than ten feet from each front line of the
 Zone Lot; provided, however, that on the two shorter dimensions
 of any block oblong in shape, the front setback may be reduced
 to five feet for electric substations, gas regulator stationsand
 utility pumping stations; and provided, further, that detached
 accessory structures and accessory uses, except garages, shall be
 set in a sufficient distance from each front line of the Zone Lot
 so that such structures and uses are located only on the rear one-
 fourth of the Zone Lot.    On a corner Zone Lot, detached accessory
 structures and accessory uses, except garages, shall be set in a
 sufficient distance from each front line of the Zone Lot so that
 such structurffi and uses are located only on the rear part of the
 Zone Lot which is adjacent to and corresponding with the rear one-
 fourth of abutting Zone Lots and are located no closer to the side
 street right-of-way than thirty feet or one-half the dimension of
 the corner Zone Lot, measured perpendicularly from such side street
right-of-way, whichever distance is greater •
    • 4-3(2)(b).   Rear Setback.     If no alley abuts the rear line of
the Zone Lot, all detached accessory structures and fixtures shall
be set in a distance of not less than five feet and all other struc-
tures shall be set in a distance of not less than twenty feet from
the rear line of the Zone Lot.       If an alley abuts the rear line of
the Zone Lot, detached garages and carports opening directly on the
alley shall be set in a distance of not less than ftve feet from
the alley line; detached accessory structures (including garages

    •4-3( 3). Bulk of Structur~ No part of any structure (except
church spires, church towers, flagpoles, antennas, chimneys, flues,
vents or accessory water tanks) shall project up through bulk limits
which are defined by planes starting at horizontal lines which are
co-directional to the front, rear and side lines of the Zone Lot
and pass through points ten feet above the midpoint of each such
front, rear and side line and which planes extend up over the Zone
Lot at an angle of sixty-three degrees and twenty-six minutes with
nspect to the horizontal (a pitch of two feet additional rise for
each foot additional setback).     (See Graphic Fig. No. 1)
    .4-3(4).   Maximum_Gross Floor Area in Structures.       The sum total
of the gross floor area of all structures on a Zone Lot, excluding
garage space, shall not be greater than three times the area of the
Zone Lot on which the structures are located •
    •4-3(5).   QlJ.iside Area of Window Exposure.    Each legally required
window shall have not less than the following amount of outside
exposure determined in the following manner:        From a reference point
located at the bottom center of the window, extendrutward at right
angle to the window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees,
centered on the window, with a radius of 10 feet.       Within this
sector the minimum required outside area of exposure for the window
shall be any open sector or combination of sectors totalling 70
degrees.    Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone Lots, the area
which may be credited as outside area of window exposure, extends
to required setback lines, regardless of theactual location of
structures.    (See Graphic Fig. No. 5)
   .4-3(6).    Minimum Size of Dwelling Unit.   The gross floor area
of a single unit dwelling and any other dwelling unit having an
individual outside entrance at ground level shall be no less than
six hundred square feet •

      .5-l.   LIMITATIONS ON_£~BNAL EFFECTS OF USES.
     ,5-1(1).    Enclosure of Uses.     Every use, unless expressly exempt-
ed by this ordinance, shall be operated in its entirety within
a completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use from the
requirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not
be enclosed" appearing after any use exempted •
     • 5-1(2).   Yolume of Sound Generated,     Every use, unless express-
ly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated that the volume
of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not exceed sixty
decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which·
the use is located •
     • 5-1(3).   Yibra±ion Generated.    Every use shall be so operated
that the ground vibration inherently and recurrently generated is
not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of any boundary
line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.

    .5-3(2)(d)(d-5).    Fences up to 42 inches in height may be erected
along any boundary line of a Zone Lot and such fences shall not be
deemed "structures" or 11 obstructions 11 for the purposes of this sec-
tion •
   • 5-3(3).   Bulk of Structures.    No part of any structure (except
church spires, church towers, flagpoles, antennas, chimneys, flues,
vents or accessory water tanks) shall project up through bulk limits
which are defined by planes starting at horizontal lines which are
co-directional to the front, rear and side lines of the Zone Lot
and pass through points twenty feet above the midpoint of each such
front, rear and side line and which planes extend up over the Zone
Lot at an angle of sixty-three degrees and twenty-six minutes with
respect to the horizontal (a pitch of two feet additional rise for
each foot additional setback).       (See Graphic Fig. No. 1)
   .5-3(4).    Maximum Gross Floor Area in Structures.     The sum total
of the gross floor area of all structures on a Zone Lot, excluding
gctrage space, shall not be greater than four times the area of the

                                     -69-

=== page 79 ===
  48 222


Zone Lot on which the structures are located •
    • 5-3(5).   Outside Area of Window Exposure.       Each legally required
window shall have not less than the following amount of outside
exposure determined in the following manner:           From a reference point
located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right
angle to the window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees,
centered on the window, with a radius of 10 feet.          Within this
sector the minimum required outside area of exposure for the window
shall be any open sector or combination of sectors totalling 70
degrees.     Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone Lots, the area
which may be credited as outside area of window exposure, extends
to required setback lines, regardless of the actual location of
structures.     (See Graphic Fig. No. 5)
    .5-3(6).    Minimum Size of Dwelling Unit.     The gross floor area
of a single unit dwelling and any other dwelling unit having an
individual outside entrance at ground level shall be no less than
six hundred square feet •

       .6-l.   LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES .
       • 6-l(l).   Enclosure of Uses.     Every use, unless expressly exempt-
ed by this ordinance, shall be operated in its entirety within a
completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use from the re-
quirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase 11 need not
be enclosed 11 appearing after any use exempted •
       • 6-1(2).   Volume of Sound Generated.     Every use, unless express-
ly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated that the volume
of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not exceed sixty
decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which
the use is located •
   • 6-1(3).       Vibration Generated.     Every use shall be so operated
that the ground vibration inherently and recurrently generated is
not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of any boundary
line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located •
   • 6-1(4).       Emission of Heat, Glare, Radiation and Fumes.     Every
use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or dan-
gerous degree of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any boundary
line of the Zon.e Lot. on whirh th0 use is located.

 of the gross floor area of the structure containing the Use by rlight .
       • 7-3.         PERMITTED STRUCTURES .
       • 7-3(1).         Zone Lot for Structures.                     A separate ground area,
 herein called the Zone Lot, shall be designated, provided and con-
 tinuously maintained for each structure containing a Use by Right.
 Every application to erect or alter such a structure shall contain
 an accurate description of the Zone Lot on which the structure is
 to be located.              Thereafter, the designated Zone Lot shall be con-
 tinuously provided and maintained for the purpose and for the
 structure designated and shall be used for no other purpose, nor
 shall the boundaries or the area of the Zone Lot be changed; pro-
vided, however, that upon application to and approval by the De-
partment of Zoning Administration, the boundaries and area of a
designated Zone Lot may be changed if, in lieu thereof, another
Lone Lot complying with all requirements herein can be designated,
provided and continuously maintained •
       . 7-3(2).        Location of Structures.                     All structures shall be set
in a distance of not less than five feet from each front line and
a distance of not less than ten feet from the rear line of the
Zone Lot; provided, however, that electric substations, gas regula-
tor stations, utility pumpingstations, detached structures and fix-
tures for the disposal of trash or garbage may be located within
five feet of the rear line of the Zone Lot.                                 The space resulting

    .9-2(3).        6££essory Uses.           Incidental only to a Use by Right,

and employe9s;

the gross floor area of the structure containing the Use by Right •

may be credited as outside area of window exposure, extends to re-
quired setback lines, regardless of the actual location of structures.

   .10-1.   LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES .
   . 10-1(1).   Enclosure of Uses.       Every use, unless expressly exemp-
ted by this ordinance, shall be operated in its entirety within a
completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use from the re-
quirement of enclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not be
anclosed 11 appearing after any use exempted •
   • 10-1(2).   Volume_Qf Sound Generated.        Every use, unless express-
ly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated that the volume
of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not exceed seven-
ty decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on
which the use is located •

not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of any boundary
line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located .

   . 10-3(2).   Bulk of Structures.     No part of any structure or fix-

extending up over the Zone Lot at an angle of 75 degrees and 4 minu-
tes with respect to the horizontal (a pitch of 3.75 feet additional
rise for each foot additional setback).             (See Graphic Fig.   ~o.   3)
   .10-3(3).    Maximum Gross Flgor Area in Structures.          The sum total

be greater than ten times the area of the Zone Lot on which the struc-
tures are located •

  .11-1.     LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES •
  • 11-1(1).     Volume of Sound Generated,     Every use, unless expressly
exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated that the volume of
sound inherently and recurrently generated does not exceed seventy
decibels at any point of any boundary line of the Zone Lot on which
the use is located •
  • 11-1(2).     Vibration Generated.     Every use shall be so operated
that the ground vibration inherently and recurrently generated is
not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of any boundary
line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located •
  • 11-1(3).     Emission of Hegt~Glare, Radiation and Fumes,      Every
use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or danger-
out degree of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any boundary
line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located •
  • 11-1(4).    Outdoor Storage and vvaste Disgosal .
  • ll-l(4)(a).      No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids or
gases shall be stored in bulk above ground; provided, however, that
tanks or drums of fuel for railroad locomotive fueling or directly
connecting with energy devices, heating devices or appliances located
on the same Zone Lot as the tanks or drums of fuel are excluded from
this provision;
  ,ll-1 ( 4) (b) •   All outdoor sto1.·age facilities for fuel, raw mater-

      .ll-l(4)(d).    All materials or wastes which might cause fumes or

   •12-3(3).    Outside Area of Window Exposure.             Each legally required
window shall have not less than the following amount of outside ex-
posuredetermined in the following manner:               From a reference point
located at the bottom center of the window, extend outward at right
angle to the window plane, a horizontal sector of 140 degrees, cena
tered on the window, with a radius of 10 feet.               Within this sector
the minimum required outside area of exposure for the window shall
be any open sector or combination of sectors totalling 70 degrees.
Applied in the direction of adjoining Zone Lots, the area which may
be credited as outside area of window exposure, extends to required
~tback   lines, regardless of the actual location of structures. (See
Graphic Fig. No. 6)

       .13-2.   PERMITTED STRUCTURES.
       ,13-2(1),   Zone Lot for Structures.   A separate ground area,
 herein called the Zone Lot, shall be designated, provided and
 continuously maintained for each structure containing a Use by
 Right.    Every application to erect or alter such a structure shall
 contain an accurate description of the Zone Lot on which the
 structure is to be located.    Thereafter, the designated Zone Lot
 shall be continuously provided and maintained for the purpose and
 for the structure designated and shall be used for no other pur-
 pose, nor shall the boundaries or the area of the Zone Lot be
 changed; provided, however, that upon application to and approval
 by the Department of Zoning Administration, the boundaries and
 area of a designated Zone Lot may be changed if, in lieu thereof,
 another Zone Lot complying with all requirements herein can be
 designated, provided and continuously maintained,
       ,13-2(2},   Location of Structures.    All structures shall be
 set in a distance of not less than twenty feet from each front,
 rear and side line of the Zone Lot; provided, however, that no
 setback shall be required for electric substations, gas regulator
 stations and utility pumping stations except from such lines of
 the Zone Lot as abut public rights-of-way.      The space resulting
 from the foregoing setbacks shall be open and unobstructed,

     .14-1.   P-1 DISTRICT •
     • 14-1(1). Uses by Right •
     • 14-2. PERMITTED SIGNS. (See Section 613.)
     .14-1. PERMITTED USES. No land shall be used or occupied
except for one only of the following Uses by Right:
     .14-1(1).     Parking lot:   a parking lot operated and maintained
in accordance with all of the following conditions:
     .14-l(l)(a).     Is not used for the sale, repair or dismantling
of any vehicles, equipment, materials or supplies;
     .14-l(l)(b).     Is properly graded for drainage; surfaced with
concrete, asphaltic concrete, asphalt, oil or any other dust-free
surfacing and maintained in good condition, free of weeds, dust,
trash or debris;
     .14-l(l)(c).     Is provided with entrances and exits so located
as to minimize traffic congestion and the effect of headlights at
night;
     .14-l(l)(d).     Is provided with wheel or bumper guards so
located and arranged that no part of any parked vehicles will ex-
tend beyond the boundaries of the parking lot;
     .14-l(l)(e).     Lighting facilities are so arranged that they
neither unreasonably disturb occupants of adjacent residential
properties nor interfere with traffic;
     .14-l(l)(f).     Has a fence on each boundary of the parking
lot which abuts a residential district; the fence is between
three and five feet high at the grade of the parking lot at the
fence line and is designed to obscure from abutting residential
districts the direct light from automobile headlights; the fence
is designed to be, and can and will be, maintained in a sightly
condition and will not be used as a support for any signs except
those signs permitted in the district;
     .14-l(l)(g).     Has only one attendant shelter building contain-
ing not more than fifty square feet of gross floor area and set
in a distance of not less than twenty feet from any boundary of

       • 16-1.      LIMITATIONS ON EXTERNAL EFFECTS OF USES •
       • 16-1(1).      Enclosure of Uses.     Every use, unless expressly ex-
empted by this ordinance, shall be operated in its entirety within
a completely enclosed structure; the exemption of a use from the
requirement of 9nclosure will be indicated by the phrase "need not
be enolosed 11 appearing after any use exempted •
       • 16-1(2).      Volume of Sound Generated.     Every use, unless ex-
pressly exempted by this ordinance, shall be so operated that ti1e
volume of sound inherently and recurrently generated does not
exceed sixty decibels at any point of any boundary line of the
Zone Lot on Which the use is located •
       • 16-1(3).      Vibration Generated.    Every use shall be so operated
that the ground vibration inherently and recurrently generated is
not perceptible, without instruments, at any point of any boundary
line of the Zone Lot on which the use is located •
       • 16-1(4).      Emission of Heat, Glare, Radiation and Fumes.     Every
use shall be so operated that it does not emit an obnoxious or
dangerous degree of heat, glare, radiation or fumes beyond any
boundary ltne of the Zone Lot on which the use is located.

      .16-1(5).       OUtdoor Storage and Waste Disposal •
      . l6-1(5){a).        No highly flammable or explosive liquids, solids
or gases shall be stored in bulk above ground; pr•ovided, however,
that tanks or drums of fuel directly connecting with heating de-
vices or appliances located on the same Zone Lot as the tanks or
drums of fuel are excluded from this provision;
      .16-l(S)(b).         All outdoor storage facilities for fuel, raw
materials and products shall be enclosed by a fence adequate to
conceal such facilities from adjacent property;
      .16-l{S)(c).         No materials or wastes shall be deposited upon a
Zone Lot in such form or manner that they may be transferred off
the Zone Lot by natural causes or forces;
      .16-l{S)(d).         All materials or wastes which might cause fumes
or dust or which constitute a fire hazard or Which may be edible
by or otherwise be attractive to rodents or insects shall be
stored outdoors only in closed containers •

by rrempora.ry     Permit.     The number enclosed in parentheses which

follows any use shall not be deemed a limitation of the use but
shall be deemed the classification or the use for the purpose of
off-street parking requirements •

             •16-2(3}.     Accessory Uses.      Incidental only to a Use by
         Right, any use which complies with all of the following condi-
         tions may be operated as an Accessory Use:
             .16-2(3}(a).     Is clearly incidental and customary to and
         commonly associated with the operation of the Use by Right;
             .16-2(3)(b).     Is operated and maintained under the same
         ownership, or by lessees or concessionaires thereof, and on the
         same Zone Lot as the Use by Right;
             .16-2(3)(c).     Does not include structures or structural
         features inconsistent with the Use by Right;
             .16-2(3)(d).     Does not include residential occupancy except
         by domestic employees employed on the premises and the immediate
         families of such employees;
             .16-2(3)(e}.     If operated partially or entirely in detached
         structures, such detached structures shall be limited to a gross
         floor area of not more than ten per cent of the area of the Zone
         Lot on which the Use by Right is located;
             .16-2(3}(f).     If operated partially or entirely within the
         structure containing the Use by Right, the grosa floor area
         within such structure utilized by Accessory Uaea (except garages
         and dining rooms for the exclusive use of occupants or persons
         employed in the structure) shall not be greater than:
             .16-2(3)(f)(f-l).     Twenty per cent of the gross floor area,
         but not to exceed three hundred square feet, of a single unit
         dwelling or any dwelling unit in a multiple unit dwelling con-
         taining eight or less dwelling units;
             .16-2(3)(f)(f-2).     Ten per cent of the gross floor area of a
         structure containing any other Use by Right.

                           613.   PgRMITTED SIGNS •

•1.   SCOPE OF REGULATIONS •
• 2.  GENERAL LIMITATIONS •
• 3.  CLASSIFICATION OF SIGNS •
. 4. PERlviiTTED USE AND LOCATION OF SIGNS •
• 5. DETERMINATION OF SIZE OF SIGNS •
• 5-l, FLaT, WALL A~D WINDOW SIGNS •
       •1.     SCOPE OF REGULAT~.     The regulations herein set forth
shall apply and govern in all districts except the B-4, B-5, I-1
and I-2 Districts in which districts these regulations shall be of
no force and effect; provided, however, that in the enumerated ex-
cluded districts no sign, outdoor commercial advertising device or
lighting device constituting a nuisance to an adjacent residential
district, because of lighting glare, focus, animation or flashing
of the sign, lighting or advertising device, shall be erected or
continued in operation •
       • 2.    GENERAL LHUTATIONS.   No sign or class or classes of signs
shall be erected or maintained unless the sign or class or classes
of signs co~plies with all of the following conditions:
       .2-1.     Is erected and maintained only by a Use by Right spe-
cifically enumerated for the district in which the sign is lo-
cated and which Use by Right is a primary use of the premises on
which the sign is located;
       .2-2.     Is clearly incidental, customary to and commonly
associated with the operation of the Use by Right;
       .2-3,    Is established and controlled under and by the same own-
ership as the Use by Right;
       .2-4.    Is limited in location to the premises on which the Use
by Right is located;
       .2-5.    Is limited in height and horizontal projection by the
same bulk control planes governing all other structures in the
district in which the sign is located and is further limited in
particular size or height by specifications in the respective and
applicable sign class;



                                      -13S'-

=== page 167 ===
 48       306


      .2-~.     Is limited in subject matter to the name, design,
picture or trademark of the owner, operator, builder, sales agent,
managing agent, lessor or lessee of the premises or of the activ-
ities (including merchandise handled or services rendered) on the
preillises on which such sign is located and does not include any
general commercial advertising unrelated to or extending in sub-
stantial degree beyond the enumerated permitted subjects •
      • 3..   Q1A~.§IFICATI0l'J   OF SIGNS,    The following classification of
signs shall be deemed to include all signs permitted in any dis-
trict.        If for any reason the classification of any sign is not
readily determinable, the classification of such sign shall be
fixed by the Department of Zoning Administration •
   •3-1.        CLA.§§_l.    The following signs meeting the following
specifications shall constitute Class 1:
   Structural Type;           Flat, wall or window sign; single face only;
   Maximum Size of Single Sign:               One hundred square inches;
   Maximum Height Above Grade at Sign:               Attached to building;
   Limitations on Kumber of Signs or Total area of Signs:                  One
sign for each street front of the Zone Lot on which the Use by
Right is located;
   Other Limitations or Conditions:               Shall be neither illuminated
nor animated •
   •3-2.        CL~ss   2.   The following signs meeting the follow i.::.1g
specifications shall constitute Class 2:
   Structur~l        Type:    Flat, wall or window sign; single faccl only;
   r1aximum Size of Single Sign:              Twelve square feet;
   t1aximum Height Above Grade at Sign:              Attached to building;
   Limitations on Number of Signs or Total Area of Signs:                  One
sign for each street front of the Zone Lot on which the Use by
Right is located;
   Other Limitations or Conditions:               Shall be neither flashing nor
animated; may be illuminated, but only from a concealed light source.

   !~ximum    Height Above Grade at Sign:    Attached to building;

   Other Limitations or Conditions:        Shall be neither flashing
ncr animated; may be illuminated, but only from a concealed light

   •3-4. CLASS 4. The following signs meeting the following
specifications shall constitute Class 4:
   Structural Type:      Individual letters; single face only;
   Maximum Size of Single Sign:      Maximum height of individual let-
ters eighteen inches;
   Maximum Height Above Grade at Sign:       Attached to building;
   Limitations on Number of Signs or Total Area of Signs:        One
sign for each street front of the Zone Lot on which the Use by
Right is located;
   Other Limitations or Conditions:        (a) Shall be neither flashing
nor animated; may be illuminated, but only from a concealed light
source;    (b) Shall be limited in subject matter to an identification
of the owner, operator, manager, lessor or lessee of the premises or
the name of the premises;      (c) Shall include no configuration,
structure, trademark, picture or design other than letters compris-
ing the permitted subject matter •
   •3-5. CLASS 2· The following signs meeting the following

    •3-9. CLAS§_2.         The following signs meeting the following

   Structural Type:        Flat, wall, window or ground sign; single

   Limitations on Number of Signs or Total Area of Signs:            The

twenty-five; provided, however, that the aggregate area of any
such sign or signs may be at least twelve square feet; and provided,

further, that no single sign shall have an area of mor~ than
fifty square feet on premises of already developed use or an area
of more than one hundred square feet on premises not developed;
     Other Limitations or Conditions:          (a) Shall not be animated;
may be illuminated, but only from a concealed light source;
(b) Are temporary only, permitted only upon application to and
issuance by the Department of Zoning Administrati('ln of a permit
therefor; application must establish a need to identify or adver-
tise new construction or the sale, lease, rental, remodeling or
rebuilding of designated structures ~n a designated land area;
permits are valid for a period of not exceeding six months and
are renewable, upon further application, for additional periods
of six months each; provided, however, that no single sign having
an area of more than twelve square feet shall be authorized for a
consecutive period of more than two years;            (c) May be. located
only on premises of, and identifying or advertising, structures
being built, sold, lAased, rented or remodeled thereon •

The duty to provide and maintain the off-street parking space
herein required shall be the joint and several responsibility of
the operator and owner of the use and the operator and owner of
the land on which or the structure or structures in which is
located the use or uses for which off-street parking space is
required to be provided and maintained.         No land shall be used or
occupied, no structure shall be designed, erected, altered, used
or occupied and no use shall be operated unless the off-street
parking space herein required is provided in at least the amount
and maintained in the manner herein set forth; provided, however,
that off-street parking space need be neither provided nor main-
tained for land, structures or uses actually used, occupied and
operated on the effective date of this ordinance unless, after the
effective date of this ordinance, such land, structures or uses
are enlarged, expanded or changed so as to require a greater amount

   .5-l.     In the R-0, R-1, R-2, R-3 and R-4 Districts, shall be

concrete, asphaltic concrete, asphalt, oil or any other dust-free
surfacing and maintained in good condition, free of weeds, dust,
trash and debris;
   .5-4.     Shall be provided with barriers of such dimensions that
occupants of adjacent structures are not unreasonably disturbed,
either by day or by night, by the movement of vehicles;
   .5-5.     Shall be provided with entrances and exits so located as
to minimize traffic congestion;
   .5-6.     Shall be provided with wheel guards or bumper guards so
located that no part of parked vehicles will extend beyond the
parking space;
   .5-7.     Lighting facilities shall be so arranged that they
neither unreasonably disturb occupants of adjacent residential
properties nor interfere with traffic;

are required to provide and maintain six hundred and forty or more

to be provided,       No structure shall be designed, erected, altered,

is provided in at least the amount herein set forth; provided,          h~w­

this ordinance unless, after the effective date of this ordinance,
such structures are enlarged or expanded, or the uses within such

shall not be used, occupied or operated unless there is provided
for the increment only of such structures, and maintained as herein
required, at least the amount of off-street loading space that would
be required hereunder if the increment were a separate structure.

   •1.     ~URPQSE    OF PROCEDURE.    Pursuant to the procedure hereinafter
set forth, two or more structures each containing one or more Uses
by Right may be erected on the same Zone Lot,            The procedure permits
diversification in the location of structures for optimum arrange-
ment of a building group on a single Zone Lot, without in any way
jeopardizing or reducing the zoning standards associated with the
public health, safety and welfare.            Under the circumstances of
topography, traffic on boundary streets, and other neighboring
conditions affecting the design, a residential Building Group Plan
provides optimum light, air, and privacy; avoids long parallel
building arrangements or direct facing on heavy traffic; provides
suitable open space for landscaping and recreation as may be reason-
ably required by the concentration of tenants,

   ,1-2.     UNIFORMITY OF DISTRICT REGULATIONS AND RESTRICTIONS.
             --------------                     ---- -        -
No amendment to this ordinance shall be adopted whereby the regu-
lations and restrictions established thereby are not uniform for
each district having the same classification and bearing the same
symbol or designation on the attached map, nor shall any amendment
to this ordinance be adopted whereby districts having the same
classification and bearing the same symbol or designation on the
attached map are subject to different regulations and restrictions •
   • 1-3.    1VII,NII>i1I.M..12JZE_OFJ:IEW_l2ISTIUQI.§.   No amendment to this or-

the district so created meets the following requirements as to
minimum size:
   .1-3(1).      An R-0 or R-1 District shall be large enough in gross
area so that at least half of the number of existing building sites
included are entirely within the interior of the district and do

District.       For the purposes of this section, all vacant land in-
cluded in the proposed district shall be divided into minimum size
building site~ permitted in the district;
     .l-3(2).      A B-1 or B-2 District shall contain at least 70,000
square feet of gross area; provided, however, that a B-1 District
which would abut a B-2, B-3, B-4, B-5, I-1 or I-2 District shall
be exempt from any requirement of minimum size;
     .1-3(3).      An R-2, R-3, R-4, B-3, B-4, B-5, I-l or I-2 District

                     . 1-1.     Words and phrases not defined herein shall be given thei1
             usual and customary mea~ings except where the context clearly in-
             dicates a different meaning .
                     . 1-2.     The text of the ordinance           shall control       captions, title
            and maps •
                     . 1-3.     JVhere any requirement of this ordinance results in a
             fraction of a unit, a fraction of five-tenths or more shall be a
            whole unit and a fraction of less than five-tenths shall be disre-
             garded •
                     • 1-4.     The word "shall" is mandatory and not permissive .
                     • l-5.     The word "may" is permissive and not mandatory .
                     . l-6.     Words used in the singular include the plural and ~ords
             used in the plural include the singular .
                     . 1-7.     Words used in the present tense include the future tense
             and words used in the future tense include the prese nt tense .
                     . 1-8.     Any word, phrase or term not herein defined but which
             word, phrase or term is defined in the Building Code of ti1e City
             and County of Denver shall be construed as defined in such code .
                     • 2.     DEFINITIONS .
                     . 2-1.     Accessory Structure:         a detached subordinate structure
             located on the same Zone Lot with the main building, the use of
             which is customary to the main building .
                     • 2-2.     Alley:   a public thoroughfare which affords only a secon-
             dary means of access to abutting property .
                     . 2-3.     Alter:   any structural change in the supporting or load-
             bearing members of a building, such as bearing walls, columns,
             beams, girders, floor joists •
                     . 2-4.     Animated Sign:      any sign having a conspicuous and inter-
             mittent variation in the illumination or physical position of any
             part of the sign.

keeping unit:      (1) any number nf persons immediately related by
blood, marriage or adoption or (2) five or fewer persons not neces-
sarily related by blood, marriage or adoption, plus, in each of the
foregoing instances, domestic servants employed for services on the
premises;     pr~vided,    however, that upon applicatien to and issuance
by the Department of Zoning Administration of a permit therefor,
one additional kitchen may be maintained solely for domestic ser-
vants, such permit to be valid for a period of one year only but
renewable annually for a like period .
   . 2-73.    Storage:      the charging of a consideration for warehousins
   .2-74.     Store:      a use devoted exclusively to the retail sale of c
commodity or c0mmodities .
   • 2-75.    Story:      that part of a building between the surface of a
floor and the ceiling immediately above •
   . 2-76.    Street:     a poblic thoroughfare, avenue, road, highway,
boulevard, parkway, way, drive, lane, court or private easement
providing the primary roadway to and egress from the property
abutting thereon .
   • 2-77.    Structural Feature:     any part of a structure which is
designed for or indicative of the intent to aclommodate any giver1 us•
   .2-78,     Structure:     anything constructed or erected, the use of
which requires location on the ground or attached to something havin~

                                      -185-

=== page 217 ===
48   356

 a location on the gro11nd, but not including fences up to 42 inches
 in height, or poles and appurtenances thereto used for the provision•
 of public utilities •
     • 2-79.   Tourist Home:     one or more buildings each building con-
 taining one or more rovms used only for transient occupancy •
     • 2-80.   Trailer camp or court:         any premises where one or more
trailer coaches are parked for living and sleeping purposes, or
 any premises used or set apart for the purpose of suppl~g to the
 public parking space for one or more trailer coaches for living and
 sleeping purposes, and which include any buildings, structures, ve-
hicles or enclosure used or intended for use as a part of the equip-
ment of such trailer camp or court,
     .2-81.    Transient Occupancy:         the rental of housing accommodations
or rooms on a day to day basis including a daily change of linen and
towels with no change in rate based on the period of occupancy.
     ,2-82.    Unobstructed Open Space:         Land with no structures thereon,
except pits for household garbage cans .
     • 2-83,   Use:   the purpose for which land or structures thereon
is designed, arranged or intended to be occupied or used, or for
which it is occupied, maintained, rented or leased •
     . 2-84.   Use by Right:     a ·use which is listed as a Use by Right
in any given zone district in this ordinance.             Uses by Right are not
required to show need for their location •
     • 2-85.   Use by Temporary Permit:         listed uses which may be per-
mitted in any given district provided that the need for the use in
me district can be established to the satisfaction of the Department
of Zoning Administration as herein provided .
     . 2-86.   Wall Sign:     any sign which is painted or otherwise depic-
ted directly upon a wall .
     • 2-87.   Warehousing:    the depositing or securing of goods, wares
and merchandise in a warehouse •
     • 2-88.   Wholesale: sale for resale not for direct consumption •
     • 2-89.   Window Sjgn:    any   ~ign   visible through a window and design-

                                        -186-

=== page 218 ===
                                                    48 357
 ed to be visible through a window or painted or otherwise affixed
 to the external surface of a window •
    . 2-90.   Zone Lot:   the land area designated as the building ~1te
 for a structure; also, the land area occupied by a use incidental
to the operation of the use.

    SECTION 2.         This ordinance is hereby ordered published in pam-
phlet form in accordance with the requirements of the Charter.                           Pub·
lication of this ordinance shall be complete the day on which two
such pamphlet forms and one copy of the official map delineating
the various districts are deposited with the City Clerk.                        As soon
as practicable thereafter, the City Clerk shall cau9e to be pub-
lished in the official newspaper a legal notice declaring publica-
tion complete.
    SECTION 3.         The Council finds this ordinance is necessary for
the immediate preservation of the public health and public safety,
and determines that it shall take effect immediately upon its final
passage and publication.
